  steps[1]  P 'Add them up and set the sum equal to 60.'

            S Add them up and set the sum equal to 60. n minus 1, plus n plus n plus 1, equals 60.
  steps[2]  P 'Simplify the left side. The -1 and +1 cancel out!'
            L '3n = 60'
            S Simplify the left side. The minus 1 and plus 1 cancel out! 3 n equals 60.
  steps[3]  P 'Divide both sides by 3 to find n.'
            L 'n = \\frac{60}{3} = 20'
            S Divide both sides by 3 to find n. n equals 60 over 3, which is 20.
  steps[4]  P "So the middle number is 20, and the three consecutive numbers are 19, 20, and 21. Let's verify: 19 + 20 + 21 = 60"
            S So the middle number is 20, and the three consecutive numbers are 19, 20, and 21. Let's verify: 19 plus 20 plus 21 equals 60.

  q         P 'Sunita is 5 years older than Kavita. The sum of their age after 10 years will be 55 years. Find the present ages of Sunita and Kavita?'
            S Sunita is 5 years older than Kavita. The sum of their age after 10 years will be 55 years. Find the present ages of Sunita and Kavita?
  options[0] P 'Kavita 10 years Sunita 15 years'
            S Kavita 10 years Sunita 15 years
  options[1] P 'Kavita 12 years Sunita 17 years'
            S Kavita 12 years Sunita 17 years
  options[2] P 'Kavita 15 years Sunita 20 years'
            S Kavita 15 years Sunita 20 years
  options[3] P 'Both are 27 1/2 years'
            S Both are 27 one half years
  hints[0]  P 'This is an age problem! Think about how their ages relate to each other now, and what happens when you add 10 years to BOTH of their ages.'
            S This is an age problem! Think about how their ages relate to each other now, and what happens when you add 10 years to both of their ages.
  hints[1]  P "Start by letting Kavita's present age be some number. Then Sunita's age is that number + 5. Now add 10 to each of their ages (for 'after 10 years') and set the total equal to 55."
            S Start by letting Kavita's present age be some number. Then Sunita's age is that number plus 5. Now add 10 to each of their ages, for after 10 years, and set the total equal to 55.
  hints[2]  P "Here's a similar problem: Ravi is 3 years older than Amit. The sum of their ages after 5 years will be 31. What are their present ages?\n\nLet Amit's age = x. Ravi's age = x + 3.\nAfter 5 years: Amit = x + 5, Ravi = x + 3 + 5 = x + 8.\nSum: (x + 5) + (x + 8) = 31 → 2x + 13 = 31 → 2x = 18 → x = 9.\nSo Amit is 9 and Ravi is 12."
            S Here's A similar problem: Ravi is 3 years older than Amit. The sum of their ages after 5 years will be 31. What are their present ages? Let Amit's age equals x. Ravi's age equals x plus 3. After 5 years: Amit equals x plus 5, Ravi equals x plus 3 plus 5, which is x plus 8. Sum: x plus 5, plus x plus 8, equals 31, so 2 x plus 13 equals 31, so 2 x equals 18, so x equals 9. So Amit is 9 and Ravi is 12.
  steps[0]  P "Let Kavita's present age be x years. Since Sunita is 5 years older, Sunita's present age is x + 5 years."
            L '\\text{Kavita} = x, \\quad \\text{Sunita} = x + 5'
            S Let Kavita's present age be x years. Since Sunita is 5 years older, Sunita's present age is x plus 5 years.
  steps[1]  P "After 10 years, Kavita's age will be x + 10 and Sunita's age will be x + 5 + 10 = x + 15."
            L '\\text{Kavita (after 10 yrs)} = x + 10, \\quad \\text{Sunita (after 10 yrs)} = x + 15'
            S After 10 years, Kavita's age will be x plus 10 and Sunita's age will be x plus 5 plus 10 equals x plus 15.
  steps[2]  P 'The sum of their ages after 10 years is 55. So we write the equation:'
            L '(x + 10) + (x + 15) = 55'
            S The sum of their ages after 10 years is 55. So we write the equation:. x plus 10, plus x plus 15, equals 55.
  steps[3]  P 'Combine like terms on the left side:'
            L '2x + 25 = 55'
            S Combine like terms on the left side:. 2 x plus 25 equals 55.
  steps[4]  P 'Subtract 25 from both sides to find 2x:'
            L '2x = 55 - 25 = 30'
            S Subtract 25 from both sides to find 2 x:. 2 x equals 55 minus 25, which is 30.
  steps[5]  P 'Divide both sides by 2:'
            L 'x = \\frac{30}{2} = 15'
            S Divide both sides by 2:. x equals 30 over 2, which is 15.
  steps[6]  P "So Kavita's present age is 15 years, and Sunita's present age is 15 + 5 = 20 years."
            S So Kavita's present age is 15 years, and Sunita's present age is 15 plus 5, which is 20 years.
  steps[7]  P "Let's verify: After 10 years, Kavita = 25 and Sunita = 30. Sum = 25 + 30 = 55. Perfect!"
            S Let's verify: After 10 years, Kavita equals 25, and Sunita equals 30. Sum equals 25 plus 30, which is 55. Perfect!

  q         P '5x + 7 = 3x + 17 then x =?'
            S 5 x plus 7 equals 3 x plus 17 then x equals what?
  options[0] P '7'
            S 7
  options[1] P '5'
            S 5
  options[2] P '10'
            S 10
  options[3] P '3'
            S 3
  hints[0]  P 'This is a simple equation where the same variable appears on both sides. Your goal is to collect all the x-terms on one side and all the numbers on the other side!'
            S This is a simple equation where the same variable appears on both sides. Your goal is to collect all the x-terms on one side and all the numbers on the other side!
  hints[1]  P 'Start by subtracting 3x from both sides to get all the x-terms together on the left side.'
            S Start by subtracting 3 x from both sides to get all the x-terms together on the left side.
  hints[2]  P "Here's a similar problem: Solve 4x + 3 = 2x + 11.\nStep 1: Subtract 2x from both sides → 2x + 3 = 11.\nStep 2: Subtract 3 from both sides → 2x = 8.\nStep 3: Divide both sides by 2 → x = 4. \nNow try the same approach with your equation!"
            S Here's A similar problem: Solve 4 x plus 3 equals 2 x plus 11. Step 1: Subtract 2 x from both sides, so 2 x plus 3 equals 11. Step 2: Subtract 3 from both sides, so 2 x equals 8. Step 3: Divide both sides by 2, so x equals 4. Now try the same approach with your equation!
  steps[0]  P 'Write down the equation.'
            L '5x + 7 = 3x + 17'
            S Write down the equation. 5 x plus 7 equals 3 x plus 17.
  steps[1]  P 'Subtract 3x from both sides to move all x-terms to the left side.'
            L '5x - 3x + 7 = 3x - 3x + 17 \\implies 2x + 7 = 17'
            S Subtract 3 x from both sides to move all x-terms to the left side. 5 x minus 3 x plus 7 equals 3 x minus 3 x plus 17 \implies 2 x plus 7, which is 17.
  steps[2]  P 'Now subtract 7 from both sides to move the numbers to the right side.'
            L '2x + 7 - 7 = 17 - 7 \\implies 2x = 10'
            S Now subtract 7 from both sides to move the numbers to the right side. 2 x plus 7 minus 7 equals 17 minus 7 \implies 2 x, which is 10.
  steps[3]  P 'Divide both sides by 2 to find x.'
            L 'x = \\frac{10}{2} = 5'
            S Divide both sides by 2 to find x. x equals 10 over 2, which is 5.
  steps[4]  P "Let's verify! Plug x = 5 back into the original equation: Left side = 5(5) + 7 = 25 + 7 = 32. Right side = 3(5) + 17 = 15 + 17 = 32. Both sides equal 32, so x = 5 is correct!"
            L '5(5) + 7 = 32 \\quad \\text{and} \\quad 3(5) + 17 = 32 \\quad'
            S Let's verify! Plug x equals 5 back into the original equation: Left side equals 5 times 5, plus 7, which is 25 plus 7, which is 32. Right side equals 3 times 5, plus 17, which is 15 plus 17, which is 32. Both sides equal 32, so x equals 5 is correct!

  options[3] P '17'
            S 17

            S Start by replacing x with 7 in the equation 16 x minus 7 y equals 77. What does 16 times 7 equal?
  hints[2]  P "Here's a similar problem: If 10a - 3b = 44 and a = 5, find b.\nStep 1: 10 × 5 - 3b = 44 → 50 - 3b = 44\nStep 2: 3b = 50 - 44 = 6\nStep 3: b = 6 ÷ 3 = 2. So b = 2!"
            S Here's A similar problem: If 10 A minus 3 b equals 44, and A equals 5, find b. Step 1: 10 times 5, minus 3 b equals 44, so 50 minus 3 b equals 44 Step 2: 3 b equals 50 minus 44, which is 6 Step 3: b equals 6 divided by 3, which is 2. So b equals 2!

            L '16(7) - 7y = 77'
            S Write the equation and substitute x equals 7.
  steps[1]  P 'Multiply 16 × 7 to simplify.'
            L '112 - 7y = 77'
            S Multiply 16 times 7 to simplify. 112 minus 7 y equals 77.
  steps[2]  P 'Now we need to isolate the term with y. Subtract 77 from both sides, or rearrange: move 7y to the right and 77 to the left.'
            L '112 - 77 = 7y'
            S Now we need to isolate the term with y. Subtract 77 from both sides, or rearrange: move 7 y to the right and 77 to the left. 112 minus 77 equals 7 y.
  steps[3]  P 'Calculate 112 - 77.'
            L '35 = 7y'
            S Calculate 112 minus 77. 35 equals 7 y.
  steps[4]  P 'Divide both sides by 7 to find y.'
            L 'y = \\frac{35}{7} = 5'
            S Divide both sides by 7 to find y. y equals 35 over 7, which is 5.
  steps[5]  P "So y = 5! Let's verify: 16 × 7 - 7 × 5 = 112 - 35 = 77 Perfect!"
            S So y equals 5! Let's verify: 16 times 7, minus 7 times 5 equals 112 minus 35, which is 77 Perfect!

  steps[1]  P "Now let's check Option A: 2p + 3q"
            L '2 \\times 5 + 3 \\times 6 = 10 + 18 = 28 \\neq 21'

  hints[2]  P "Here's a similar problem: If a = 4 and b = 5, which expression is greater than 15?\n• 2a + b = 2(4) + 5 = 13 \n• 3a + b = 3(4) + 5 = 17 \nSo 3a + b would be the answer since 17 > 15!"
            S Here's A similar problem: If A equals 4, and b equals 5, which expression is greater than 15? • 2 A plus b equals 2 times 4, plus 5, which is 13 • 3 A plus b, which is 3 times 4, plus 5, which is 17 So 3 A plus b would be the answer since 17 > 15!

            L '2 + 3 - 3 = 2'

  options[3] P 'x + 3y'
            S x plus 3 y
  hints[0]  P 'This is a problem about the **distributive property** — when you multiply a number by a group of things added together inside parentheses, you need to share that multiplication with EVERY term inside!'
            S This is a problem about the **distributive property**, when you multiply a number by a group of things added together inside parentheses, you need to share that multiplication with every term inside!

  options[1] P 'a - b + 2c'

            S None of these

  hints[1]  P 'Start by substituting a = 2, b = 3, and c = 1 into each option one by one. For example, for option A: replace a with 2, b with 3, and c with 1, then calculate.'
            S Start by substituting A equals 2, b equals 3, and c equals 1 into each option one by one. For example, for option A: replace a with 2, b with 3, and c with 1, then calculate.
  hints[2]  P "Here's a similar problem: If x = 4 and y = 2, which expression equals zero?\n• x + y → 4 + 2 = 6 (not zero)\n• x - 2y → 4 - 2(2) = 4 - 4 = 0 \nSo x - 2y would be the answer! Same idea — just substitute and check each one."
            S Here's A similar problem: If x equals 4, and y equals 2, which expression equals zero? • x plus y, so 4 plus 2 equals 6, not zero, • x minus 2 y, so 4 minus 2 times 2 equals 4 minus 4, which is 0 So x minus 2 y would be the answer! Same idea, just substitute and check each one.
  steps[0]  P "We're given a = 2, b = 3, and c = 1. Let's substitute these values into each expression one by one."

  steps[1]  P 'Option A: a + b + c'
            L '2 + 3 + 1 = 6'
            S Option A: A plus b plus c. 2 plus 3 plus 1 equals 6.
  steps[2]  P "6 is not zero, so Option A is not our answer. Let's try Option B."
            S 6 is not zero, so Option A is not our answer. Let's try Option B.
  steps[3]  P 'Option B: a - b + 2c'
            L '2 - 3 + 2(1) = 2 - 3 + 2 = 1'
            S Option B: a minus b plus 2 c. 2 minus 3 plus 2 times 1 equals 2 minus 3 plus 2, which is 1.

            S 1 is not zero either. Let's try Option C!

            S Option C: 2 c plus 2 A minus 2 b. 2 times 1, plus 2 times 2, minus 2 times 3 equals 2 plus 4 minus 6, which is 0.
  steps[6]  P "We got zero! Option C gives us exactly 0. That's our answer!"
            S We got zero! Option C gives us exactly 0. That's our answer!
  steps[7]  P 'Notice something cool: 2c + 2a - 2b = 2(c + a - b) = 2(1 + 2 - 3) = 2(0) = 0. You can factor out the 2 to make it even simpler!'
            L '2(c + a - b) = 2(1 + 2 - 3) = 2 \\times 0 = 0'
            S Notice something cool: 2 c plus 2 A minus 2 b equals 2 times, open bracket, c plus A minus b, close bracket, which is 2 times, open bracket, 1 plus 2 minus 3, close bracket, which is 2 times 0, which is 0. You can factor out the 2 to make it even simpler!

            S 21
  options[3] P '6'
            S 6

            S This is a substitution problem, you know the formula and some values, so plug them in to find the missing one!
  hints[1]  P 'Start by substituting a = 3 and b = 18 into the equation a = b/c. That gives you 3 = 18/c. Now solve for c!'
            S Start by substituting A equals 3, and b equals 18 into the equation A, which is b over c. That gives you 3 equals 18 over c. Now solve for c!
  hints[2]  P "Here's a similar problem: If x = y/z, x = 5, and y = 20, find x + z.\nPlug in: 5 = 20/z → z = 20 ÷ 5 = 4.\nSo x + z = 5 + 4 = 9. Now try the same approach with your numbers!"
            S Here's A similar problem: If x equals y over z, x equals 5, and y equals 20, find x plus z. Plug in: 5 equals 20 over z, so z equals 20 divided by 5, which is 4. So x plus z equals 5 plus 4, which is 9. Now try the same approach with your numbers!

            L 'a = \\frac{b}{c}, \\quad a = 3, \\quad b = 18'
            S Write down the equation and the values you know. A equals b over c, \quad A equals 3, \quad b equals 18.
  steps[1]  P 'Substitute a = 3 and b = 18 into the equation.'
            L '3 = \\frac{18}{c}'
            S Substitute A equals 3, and b equals 18 into the equation.
  steps[2]  P 'To find c, think: what number do I divide 18 by to get 3? Multiply both sides by c, then divide.'
            L '3 \\times c = 18 \\implies c = \\frac{18}{3} = 6'
            S To find c, think: what number do I divide 18 by to get 3? Multiply both sides by c, then divide. 3 times c equals 18 \implies c, which is 18 over 3, which is 6.
  steps[3]  P 'Now that we know c = 6, calculate a + c.'

  options[1] P '24'
            S 24
  options[2] P '48'
            S 48
  options[3] P 'None of these'
            S None of these
  hints[0]  P 'This is a substitution problem — plug in the values you know into the equation and solve for the unknown!'
            S This is a substitution problem, plug in the values you know into the equation and solve for the unknown!
  hints[1]  P 'Start by substituting a = 24 and c = 1/2 into the equation a = b × c. So you get 24 = b × (1/2). Now solve for b!'
            S Start by substituting A equals 24, and c equals one half into the equation A, which is b times c. So you get 24 equals b times one half. Now solve for b!
  hints[2]  P "Here's a similar problem: If x = y × z, and x = 10, z = 1/5, find y - x.\nStep 1: 10 = y × (1/5)\nStep 2: y = 10 ÷ (1/5) = 10 × 5 = 50\nStep 3: y - x = 50 - 10 = 40"
            S Here's A similar problem: If x equals y times z, and x equals 10, z equals 1 over 5, find y minus x. Step 1: 10 equals y times 1 over 5, Step 2: y equals 10 divided by 1 over 5, which is 10 times 5, which is 50 Step 3: y minus x equals 50 minus 10, which is 40.
  steps[0]  P 'Write down what we know: a = b × c, where a = 24 and c = 1/2.'
            L 'a = b \\times c'
            S Write down what we know: A equals b times c, where A equals 24, and c equals one half.
  steps[1]  P 'Substitute the known values into the equation.'
            L '24 = b \\times \\frac{1}{2}'
            S Substitute the known values into the equation. 24 equals b times one half.
  steps[2]  P 'To find b, divide both sides by 1/2. Dividing by 1/2 is the same as multiplying by 2!'
            L 'b = 24 \\div \\frac{1}{2} = 24 \\times 2 = 48'
            S To find b, divide both sides by one half. Dividing by one half is the same as multiplying by 2! b equals 24 divided by one half, which is 24 times 2, which is 48.
  steps[3]  P 'Now calculate b - a.'
            L 'b - a = 48 - 24 = 24'
            S Now calculate b minus a. b minus A equals 48 minus 24, which is 24.

  q         P 'In the formula L = M * N, If M = 13 and L = 78. Find the value of N.'
            S In the formula L equals M * N, If M equals 13, and L equals 78. Find the value of N.
  options[0] P '91'
            S 91
  options[1] P '6'
            S 6
  options[2] P '1,014'
            S 1,014
  options[3] P '5'
            S 5
  hints[0]  P "This is a 'find the missing number' problem! You're given a multiplication formula and two of the three values. Think about which operation is the opposite of multiplication."
            S This is a find the missing number problem! You're given a multiplication formula and two of the three values. Think about which operation is the opposite of multiplication.
  hints[1]  P 'Start by plugging in the values you know: L = 78 and M = 13. So the equation becomes 78 = 13 × N. Now, to find N, divide both sides by 13!'
            S Start by plugging in the values you know: L equals 78, and M equals 13. So the equation becomes 78, which is 13 times N. Now, to find N, divide both sides by 13!
  hints[2]  P "Here's a similar problem: If A = B × C, and B = 12 and A = 60, then 60 = 12 × C. To find C, we divide: C = 60 ÷ 12 = 5. See how division helps us find the missing number?"
            S Here's A similar problem: If A equals B times C, and B equals 12, and A equals 60, then 60 equals 12 times C. To find C, we divide: C equals 60 divided by 12, which is 5. See how division helps us find the missing number?
  steps[0]  P 'Write down the formula and plug in the values we know.'
            L 'L = M \\times N \\implies 78 = 13 \\times N'
            S Write down the formula and plug in the values we know. L equals M times N \implies 78, which is 13 times N.
  steps[1]  P 'To find N, we need to undo the multiplication. The opposite of multiplication is division! So divide both sides by 13.'
            L 'N = \\frac{78}{13}'
            S To find N, we need to undo the multiplication. The opposite of multiplication is division! So divide both sides by 13. N equals 78 over 13.
  steps[2]  P "Now let's do the division: How many times does 13 go into 78? Think of your 13 times table: 13 × 1 = 13, 13 × 2 = 26, 13 × 3 = 39, 13 × 4 = 52, 13 × 5 = 65, 13 × 6 = 78. Perfect!"
            L 'N = \\frac{78}{13} = 6'
            S Now let's do the division: How many times does 13 go into 78? Think of your 13 times table: 13 times 1 equals 13, 13 times 2 equals 26, 13 times 3 equals 39, 13 times 4 equals 52, 13 times 5 equals 65, 13 times 6 equals 78. Perfect!
  steps[3]  P "Let's verify: L = M × N = 13 × 6 = 78. That matches! So N = 6."
            S Let's verify: L equals M times N, which is 13 times 6, which is 78. That matches! So N equals 6.

  hints[1]  P 'Start by cross-multiplying: 625 × 1 = 25 × x. Now solve for x first, then multiply by 4.'
